## Notes for the port
### Top bar (renderer)
- IPC the page calls (main lane must provide): topbar.hello, topbar.menuState, topbar.menus, topbar.uia, topbar.invoke, topbar.uiaInvoke, topbar.window, topbar.newWindow, topbar.power, topbar.about, topbar.settings, topbar.winSettings, topbar.overGames, topbar.hidden, topbar.taskmgr, topbar.statsDetail, topbar.pingTest, topbar.pingTests, taskmgr.open, glass.topbarMenu, notes.toggle, timer.start/stopwatch/pause/resume/add/cancel, layouts.restore/save/rename/delete, apppower.set/remember/trim/reset.
- Events the page listens to: topbar.focus, .title, .geometry, .uia, .stats, .note, .close, .peek, .state, .backdrop, .guard, .under, .pingtest, .pingtests, timer.state, notes.state, layouts.state, plus the window event mantle-theme.
- Sidecar files the renderer depends on through main: sidecar/MenuBar.cs (focus snapshot, Win32/UIA menus; checks the window title), sidecar/TopbarStats.cs (1 s sample), sidecar/TopbarPing.cs + sidecar/ping-targets.json, sidecar/PingTest.cs.
- The window grows to the menu: the bar window is 28 px closed (Windows' floor is 38 DIP, shaped) and is resized to the open menu's height; dropdowns are hidden until the window is tall enough, with a 1.5 s give-up. A WinUI port with flyouts/popups in their own windows may drop this whole dance, but must keep "never a clipped slice" and the "cut off" note.
- Mac-style behaviours that are easy to lose: slide across titles while open, » overflow with real submenus, mnemonic/first-letter jump, double-click is not two confirms (300 ms), arm expires after 4 s.
- Fit order is the user's (2026-09-25): app name ellipsizes first (min 96 px), then menus move into » from the right. Stats are never shrunk and must end at the SCREEN edge, not the window edge.
- Stats 1 s update rate is the user's explicit ask; the renderer must patch text in place (no layout per tick) and main holds back identical samples with a 5 s heartbeat (stats-view.js pushGate is shared with main).
- Detail renewal is offset 450 ms after a sample on purpose: an ask answered right after a sample measured noise and rows jumped.
- App power: the order freeze (panel open / pointer over / key held) and keeping the held row even after it leaves the top five are explicit fixes; panels must not collapse on every 1 s sample.
- Dark glass and the colour-under are the hardest parts natively: both rely on a page-side canvas, CSS backdrop-filter of a supplied picture, and CSS masks; in WinUI 3 the natural route is a real acrylic/backdrop brush, which changes the contrast-solve design. Candidate for redesign rather than a literal port; the contrast guarantees (ink-2 >= 4.5:1 / 4.7 guard) are the contract.
- The colour-under "NO FADE" is the user's 2026-10-11 ask ("changes colors faster like imediatly"): ink flip and paint must land in the same frame.
- Every failure has words (NO SILENT FAILURES): glass, darkening and colour-under failures add an info row at the top of the Mantle menu AND a one-time strip note.
- The ping test history file is main's (<userData>/ping-tests.json via lib/features/_pingtest-history.js); the ping test itself runs in the sidecar and survives the dropdown closing.
- CSP blocks inline style attributes: the page sets core-bar heights and widths through the CSSOM. Not relevant natively, but the Electron tests check it.
- Light vs dark: light dropdowns animate in and use 24 px rows / 12.5 px text; dark has Helm metrics (30 px rows, 13 px), no animation, glass on a picture. A theme switch with a menu open must re-measure.
- Self-test harness lib/features/_topbar-selftest.js (Electron --test) drives most live behaviours; ui/topbar/shoot.js is a headless-Chromium check harness over mock-mantle.js (CPU-heavy: cloudtest).
### Top bar (main process)
- Sidecar files this surface depends on: sidecar/MenuBar.cs (topbar.watch/focus/menus/uia/invoke/uiaInvoke/window/guard/reserve/release, focus snapshots, game verdict), sidecar/TopbarStats.cs (topbar.stats, `topbarstats` events), sidecar/TopbarPing.cs (+ sidecar/ping-targets.json), sidecar/PingTest.cs (topbar.pingtest, `pingtest` events), sidecar/UnderRow.cs (underrow), sidecar/UnderDup.cs (underwatch, `underrow` events), sidecar/UnderPaint.cs (underpaint, underhint), sidecar/Backdrop.cs (backdrop PNG fallback), sidecar/WidgetCover.cs (widgethide.check), sidecar/GameMode.cs (gamemode.scan procs), sidecar/Taskbar.cs (AppBars.Reserve re-sends ABM_NEW), sidecar/Foreground.cs (the shared full-screen definition); also `jumplist.shortcut`, `winproc`, `focus`, `ping` verbs.
- The app bar is TOP, sized in the sidecar's units (physical px when DPI-aware). Native port must keep the 300 ms work-area check, the 6 s / 20 s pending grace, the 1.5 s and 6 s re-reserves, the 30 s re-register cap and the 2 s SELF_MS self-change ignore, or maximised windows jump several times per scale change (the 2026-10-12 fix).
- Session-end release must be written in the same tick the session-end message arrives (WM_QUERYENDSESSION/WM_ENDSESSION); the user's logs had "app bar was not released" warnings on every restart before it.
- Electron-specific workarounds that a WinUI 3 window may not need but must be re-verified: the ~38-40 DIP minimum height (cropped with a shape/region), resize bands on frameless resizable windows (hit-test must be CLIENT everywhere, 2026-09-27), Chromium moving windows into the work area (AdjustToFit) which caused the drift fight, per-origin page zoom.
- The drift fight never gives up (backoff 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, then every 30 s) and pauses while hidden; it must queue the re-check BEFORE re-placing, because setBounds fires move/resize synchronously (a 250 ms check once replaced the backoff).
- Borderless full screen is NOT fitted: a Chromium full-screen window snaps back within 1.5 s; the bar stays over its top 28 DIP. Only games (verdict or running), the lock screen, full-screen video and the manual hide step it aside.
- Known-game-running releases the strip (Valorant came up as a maximised window under the strip, 2026-09-26); video keeps it. `overGames` applies to the sidecar verdict only, never to a running known game or the lock screen.
- Peek timings: 2 DIP band, 100 ms poll, 500 ms dwell, 600 ms grace; starts disarmed; button reads via GetAsyncKeyState of all 5 buttons (physical, so a swapped mouse works); hot corners claim a 6x6 DIP square. User-requested 2026-09-27.
- Under-colour cadences (user asked for "no delay" three times, 2026-10-11): 66/125/300 ms poll, 16 ms min gap, 33 ms follow for 150 ms, frame watch with 33/100/500 ms push spacing, 1 s keep-alive (sidecar stops after 3 s), native layer painting under the bar. A native port could paint the colour in-process (no layer window needed) but must keep the frame-driven path and the protected-black and secure-desktop rules.
- The ink choice, hysteresis (+-3 levels around the crossover) and per-item contrast (>= 4.5:1) of the under colour live in the renderer (ui/topbar/under.js), not here; the renderer lane must carry those rows.
- Glass: dark only, 250 ms / 2 s after 8 identical pictures, 3 fails -> flat tint with reason, 5 s retry. The picture comes from the desktop layer page's own frame (capturePage), not screen capture: a native port with a native desktop layer needs another way to get that strip (or composition backdrop). Candidate for a WebView2 fallback only if the desktop layer stays web.
- The shared top-band alpha (look guard) is one value for Mantle, Quartz and Helm; the bar must use it in dark with look sync on, and fall back to its own with a said reason.
- Top-row hints: pipe protocol v1 (look-sync CONTRACT.md); sender identity checked by exe path rules that include worktree folders (~\browser-*, ~\helm-*); the --test pipe name must differ from live's.
- Log privacy rule: no app, game, window class or app id in log lines; the native port's logger must keep that.
- Test-only switches: MANTLE_TOPBAR_SELFTEST=1, MANTLE_TOPBAR_TEST_GAME=<exe,...>, MANTLE_TOPBAR_TEST_PIN_FOCUS=1 (keeps a test bar on its first app so a real app switch cannot close its menus, 2026-10-17), MANTLE_TOPBAR_UNDER=0 / MANTLE_TOPBAR_UNDER_HOLD=1, MANTLE_TEST_VISIBLE=1, LOOK_SYNC_TOPROW_PIPE. A --test instance: passive sidecar, no topmost, no reserve, dry-run power/new window/Task Manager/Settings, UI Automation actions refused (one took the user's keyboard, 2026-09-24).
- Many rows are verified only by lib/features/_topbar-selftest.js, which runs in a real --test Electron instance (CPU-heavy; cloudtest). Native needs an equivalent in-process self-test.
### Desktop layer
- The strip in desktop.js and lib/state.js problems() are two copies of one health rule and have already drifted: the desktop strip does NOT show taskbar watch, edge clip, taskbar hiding, bar/launcher backdrop or notifications rows that problems() has, and its Win key rule lacks problems()' `!hotkey.resting` exemption (so a resting Win key would show here). Decide in the port whether to unify (recommended: one function).
- Clock and 5 s stats park only after the cover fade has fully finished (computed visibility hidden), and uncovering paints in the same task before .covered is removed. Chromium's occlusion detection is turned off in main.js, so "page hidden" never fires under a maximised window; widgethide is the real "covered" signal. A native port must replicate "never show a stale second".
- Mission Control is a live web page in an iframe at half scale: in WinUI 3 this needs WebView2 (fallback candidate). Web wallpapers (sandboxed iframe on mantle-wp:// with the Lively shim and postMessage protocol) also need WebView2.
- Live desktop panes over a playing video wallpaper use a real backdrop blur of the page's own content; natively this requires composition (the wallpaper drawn in the same visual tree, or a Win2D/Composition blur), not DWM acrylic, which would blur Explorer's wallpaper instead.
- DefView (Explorer's icons) is above the layer and takes every click, so the panes are never clickable in live mode; the right-click menu exists only via the DeskClick.cs low-level mouse hook. Keep the hook cheap: every mouse event in Windows waits on it.
- The menu never takes the keyboard; keys reach it because the sidecar hands over bare Up/Down/Home/End/Enter only while it is the newest pop-up (lib/popwatch.js MENU KEYS).
- Widgets off is stored in the page's localStorage (`mantle.desktop.widgets`), not in Mantle settings: migrate that value to the native settings store or it resets.
- Multi-monitor: one layer on the primary display only. Widget rects are virtual-screen physical pixels; desktop window origin added before the DIP-to-physical conversion.
- Dark readability guard is computed on an eighth-scale canvas; it never runs on a timer and never in light.
- User requests cited: 12-hour clock with seconds (2026-09-21); same theme as the taskbar (2026-09-21); Mantle's own desktop menu with Shift for Windows' (2026-10-15); widgets hide under windows (2026-10-10); pane edge ring fix (2026-10-10).
- Sidecar deps: Desktop.cs (attach / recheck / detach / fit), DeskClick.cs (deskmenu.arm/ack/nack/status/testclick), WidgetCover.cs (widgethide.watch/check), Windows.cs (WinEvent hooks feeding WidgetCover), plus main's `net.probe` (lib/net-probe.js) and `sys.stats`.
### Wallpapers window (renderer)
Abbreviations: W = ui/wallpapers/wallpapers.js, H = ui/wallpapers/index.html, C = ui/wallpapers/wallpapers.css, S = ui/wallpapers/lively-shim.js, T = ui/wallpapers/wallpapers.test.js.
Renderer IPC used: `wallpaper.library` (push, M.on), `wallpaper.list` (fallback pull), `wallpaper.set`, `wallpaper.shuffle`, `wallpaper.creditOpen`, `wallpaper.setPaused`, `wallpaper.remove`, `wallpaper.undoRemove`, `wallpaper.add`, `wallpaper.reimport`, `wallpaper.props`, `wallpaper.prop`, `wallpaper.propsReset`, `wallpaper.thumb`, `wallpaper.window`; preload helper `M.pathForFile(file)`; window event `mantle-theme`.
- Scope items the brief listed that DO NOT EXIST in this UI (so no rows, but the coordinator should not expect them): URL/web-address import, per-monitor / per-display choice, a separate preview mode (only the static large thumb), set-as-Windows-wallpaper and lock-screen controls, context menus, a light-vs-dark-specific style. If those exist they live in main (lib/wallpaper*.js) or Settings, not here.
- The property editor is built once per focused item and NOT rebuilt on listing pushes (a push mid-drag would tear the slider away); live drags are throttled to 100 ms and not saved, release saves. Preserve this split (`persist:false` vs `persist:true`).
- Render-in-place: cards are kept in a Map and only re-ordered when order changes, so keyboard focus survives the frequent `wallpaper.library` pushes (runtime state changes push often).
- Remove has Undo instead of "are you sure" (2026-09-29); the Undo window length comes from main (`undoMs`, default 20 s); a newer notice cancels the auto-hide timer.
- Built-in view (2026-10-09, user asked for the same background presets as Quartz and Helm): never mixed into the user's own library; 23 Unsplash photos; credit must be shown (Unsplash License) under the grid and in details.
- Theme rule (2026-09-21, user quote): every surface wears the taskbar island glass exactly; dark via `data-theme="dark"` (2026-10-08). The blurred current-wallpaper ground under a near-opaque tint is the window's material; WinUI can do this with an Image + blur effect brush, but Mica/Acrylic would be WRONG (the material is the wallpaper itself, not the screen behind).
- Thumbnail + codec check runs in the renderer with Chromium's decoder; natively, the "can this video play" check must match whatever player the native desktop wallpaper uses (MediaPlayer / Media Foundation), otherwise the card's "codec Chromium cannot decode" wording and behaviour change. The error strings name Chromium.
- Lively shim is a JS file injected into each HTML wallpaper; it stays JavaScript even in a native port (HTML wallpapers need WebView2). Rows WALL-111..120 are WebView2-hosted by necessity; in WebView2 the parent-postMessage channel becomes `chrome.webview.postMessage` or an injected iframe host page, and `cursor` forwarding must be fed from a native mouse hook.
- Esc closes the window only when the notice is hidden, and is not hijacked inside text inputs/selects.
- Narrow layout breakpoint 880px hides the large preview and the under-grid credit (credit is in details instead).
- Tests: ui/wallpapers/wallpapers.test.js is a headless-Chromium page test with a fake window.mantle (prints "WALLPAPERS PAGE TEST: n passed, m failed"; supports MANTLE_THEME=dark|light). It does NOT exercise add results, folder add, reimport, the Customise editor, the pause-rules/Lively sections, thumbnail making, Minimise, or the shim; those rows are `none` or point at lib/wallpaper.test.js (main side only).
### Wallpaper engine (main process)
- No `wallpaper.*` settings keys exist. All persisted state is `<userData>/wallpapers/library.json`; the native port must read this file and its item folders as is (data continuity). Keep the `.removed/` and `.broken-<ms>` conventions.
- Commands the UI calls (IPC contract to keep): wallpaper.open, list, set, shuffle, creditOpen, remove, undoRemove, setPaused, add, props, prop, propsReset, reimport, thumb, status, window. Events pushed: `wallpaper`, `wallpaper.library`, `wallpaper.pause`, `wallpaper.prop`, `wallpaper.sysinfo`, `wallpaper.cursor`, all with replay.
- Hidden timings: rule poll 1000 ms; fg budget 1500 ms / 4000 ms for 5000 ms after helper readyAt; one late answer tolerated; library push coalesce 60 ms; cursor feed 66 ms; sysinfo 1000 ms; undo 20 s; rename retry 12 x 250 ms; zip timeout 120 s; still recheck 1000 ms; app-window hold 250 ms (cold 50 ms); look-sync wait 20 s; afterWindows recheck 1500 ms; sidecar timeouts perso.background/perso.lock 8 s, perso.set 15 s, perso.lockPicture 30 s.
- Ordering/race fixes: remove pushes the desktop off the file BEFORE moving the folder; the current Lively wallpaper imports first; look-desktop writes the still before the record; a newer still ask cancels an older one (gen counter); one-late-answer rule never un-pauses behind a full-screen app on an unknown.
- Work-area cover test uses physical px with a 4 px tolerance (auto-hide taskbar); primary display only throughout (desktop layer, stills, rules).
- HARD in WinUI 3: web wallpapers (Lively HTML packages) need a browser engine with a custom scheme, CORS, range requests, a Lively shim injected into <head>, livelyPropertyListener / livelySystemInformation / cursor feeds: WebView2 (SetVirtualHostNameToFolderMapping or WebResourceRequested) is the fallback candidate. Video wallpaper with CSS-like filters (saturate/hue/brightness/contrast) and object-fit modes: MediaPlayerElement lacks hue/saturation filters; a Win2D/Composition effect chain or WebView2 would be needed. Video decode failure detection (thumbnail draw = decode proof) must be re-done natively.
- Stills: native can read the frame directly from its own media pipeline instead of canvas readback; keep the source priority (own frame, look-sync still, Windows backdrop) and plain reasons.
- Sidecar dependencies by name: `foreground` (fullscreen bool, rect, exe, pid, title, hwnd), `edgeclip` event, `desktop` (recheck), `perso.background`, `perso.set` (keys picture, fit) in SysPerso.cs, `perso.lock` / `perso.lockPicture` in SysPersoLock.cs (needs the WinRT build; otherwise read-only reason), sidecar.readyAt/status/blockedReason.
- User decisions cited in code: 2026-09-22 "make it so this fully replaces lively so i can add new videos and still have my library of videos i have on lively rn"; 2026-10-09 built-in presets same as Quartz and Helm; 2026-10-09 dark see-through "look just like helm"; 2026-10-10 Windows wallpaper set and Helm/Quartz see straight to it; 2026-10-10 lock screen matches everything; remove uses Undo, not "are you sure".
- Privacy: logs never carry titles, paths or window titles (scrub); credit links only from the catalog; network-share paths refused before opening (credential leak).
- Test-only hooks: MANTLE_WP_UNDO_MS, MANTLE_WALL_PROBE (tools/wallpaper-probe.js, live test instance), S.override play/pause.
### Hot corners
- Hot-corner geometry is DIP everywhere (Electron's cursor point and display bounds). Native must convert physical pixels per monitor with per-monitor DPI, and keep the `<=` far-side rule for fractional scales (175%: 1920 px = 1097.1 DIP).
- The 6 DIP zone was sized for the bottom-edge ClipCursor (0,0,W,H-2) that keeps the cursor 2 px short of the bottom row; if the native bar changes the clip, re-check the bottom corners at 100/125/150/175/200%.
- Disarm-on-start, re-arm-only-on-leave, and "pressed bit on arrival is ignored" are subtle state-machine rules; port `step()` as a pure function and reuse the corners.test.js cases verbatim.
- 2026-10-14 bug fix: Electron's getCursorScreenPoint returns a point even when GetCursorPos fails (lock screen PIN box / UAC desktop); Task view fired after every unlock. Native must treat GetCursorPos FALSE as "no point".
- Corners claim their squares from the bottom bar's reveal band and the top bar's peek; the native bar and top bar must consult the same claim table, or a push into a corner will both fire and reveal.
- Only one gamemode.scan per fire (1500 ms timeout), not per poll; buttons are read only inside an active square. Keep the poll at 100 ms and at zero cost when everything is off (a low-level mouse hook is an option natively, but must keep the same dwell semantics).
- Task view goes through the Win+Tab feature handler (no synthetic key); Show desktop goes through Shell.Application ToggleDesktop (never Win+D); Lock goes through LockWorkStation in-process.
- Quick settings action is a round trip to the bar page with a 1500 ms ack; natively this becomes a direct call but must still report "did not open" with a reason.
- No visual indicator at the corner is drawn by Electron Mantle; do not add one without the user asking.
- Displays are re-read every 2 s by polling rather than by a display-change listener (leak avoidance).
- Sidecar dependencies: HotCorners.cs (`hotcorners.desktop`, `{dryRun}`; refused in --passive), GameMode scan (`gamemode.scan {procs:true}`). Corners.cs is unrelated (DWM window corner rounding), named here only because it shares the word. EdgeClip.cs matters for bottom-corner reachability.
- lib/corner.js is NOT hot corners: it is the bottom-right occupant registry used by toasts, screenshot card and clips record panel (covered by those lanes in depth).
### Look (theme, accent, look.json, guard)
- Speed targets: own `theme.set` -> every page within 1 s; another app's write -> every page within 1 s (look-sync debounce 50 ms, was 150, chosen for the 1 s target); newer-schema state within 1 s. Theme attribute must be on `<html>` before first paint (sync IPC answered from memory). Native: every window must pick its theme/brushes before first frame and switch live with no reload.
- Order at start: sidecar.start, then getTheme(), then getLook().start() (may set theme from look.json), then windows; startLookDesktop() runs after lib/wallpaper.js has started.
- Look-sync is VENDORED (look-sync project, commits 0b55b9c / 4f82c4b for accent.js); Helm and Quartz run the same module on the same file. A native port must keep byte-compatible JSON, the same validation, revs and atomic-rename write protocol, or the other two apps break. Its own unit tests live in the look-sync project, not in this repo.
- Race fixes: writeDesktop runs a queued `_check` before publishDesktop so a remote change is applied before the re-read; `readOnlyNow()` re-reads the file instead of trusting the last status (10-10 OPEN item); the applied-rev map is cleared when the doc rev goes down (re-seed); torn-read retry 250 ms; Windows folder deletion floods rename events with an absolute path, which closes the watcher immediately.
- Test isolation (2026-10-10 incident): a test instance wrote into the user's real look-sync folder and live Quartz followed it; --test must use LOOK_SYNC_DIR or a profile-local folder.
- The light look ignores the accent hue (user decision 2026-10-10); light glass fields map onto different tokens than dark (one white `--lg-fill`, no `--lg-fill-bar`).
- Guard band 100 DIP is a promise to the user (snow-peak stays .50). The guard solve is CPU heavy (300-600 ms on an i3): keep it off the UI thread natively.
- The guard depends on ui/shared/glass-adapt.js (glassPicture, guardRect, glassTint, gaussian); the native port needs a bit-identical port of that math (tests demand equality with glass-adapt over the whole picture).
- Stills need: JPEG encode q90, GIF first-frame decode (lib/gif-frame.js), video frame grab (lib/wall-still.js frame()), web page capture (capturePage). WebView2 CapturePreviewAsync is the likely native equivalent for web wallpapers.
- Fallback toast reuses ui/ontop page (the keep-on-top toast); 9 s life, 30 s and hidden in tests.
- Sidecar deps: SysPerso.cs (`perso.background`, `perso.set` picture/fit via IDesktopWallpaper), SysPersoLock.cs (`perso.lock`, `perso.lockPicture`, WinRT LockScreen, Ext .jpg/.jpeg/.png/.bmp), the desktop-layer `desktop {recheck}` verb.
- nativeTheme.themeSource must never be set (it would blind Match Windows); a native port reads AppsUseLightTheme directly (UISettings.ColorValuesChanged or registry watch).

## Not covered
### Top bar (renderer)
- ui/topbar/mock-mantle.js, apppower-bench.js and shoot.js internals (skipped by scope; shoot.js used only for test names).
- ui/shared/glass-adapt.js and glass-light.css token values (shared lane): only referenced by name here.
- Exact pixel values of every light-theme CSS rule (row heights, paddings) beyond the ones listed; the stat-cell min widths and main metrics are covered.
- pingtest.css visual details beyond amber/better colours (grid layout, progress bar styling) not inventoried row by row.
- lib/features/_timer-core.js parseDuration accepted formats and error wording (timer lane).
- How main decides peek, hide, game step-aside and the strip reserve (main-process lane); only the renderer side is here.
### Top bar (main process)
- ui/topbar/** (the page: menus, keyboard, the quick-settings row text, ink/hysteresis, stats formatting, ping-test UI) is the renderer lane's; only the main-side pushes it depends on are listed here.
- sidecar .cs internals (MenuBar.cs menu reading, UnderDup.cs/UnderPaint.cs prediction cache, PingTest.cs phases, TopbarPing.cs target list) were read only from their headers; their own behaviours belong to a sidecar lane.
- lib/features/_game-watch.js, _corners.js, _tools-hub.js, _pingtest-history.js and lib/apppower-core.js were only read where the top bar calls them; their full behaviour is other lanes' scope.
- No separate tray/system menu or extra shortcut exists in this scope beyond the feature's tray on/off and Win+Alt+T; the Mantle menu items themselves are rendered by ui/topbar.
### Desktop layer
- main.js's desktop backdrop capture pipeline (presentDesktop, reshootDesktopBackdrop, lib/wall-still.js) and lib/wallpaper.js decisions (what plays, pause rules): only the page side is here; owned by the wallpaper / main lanes.
- lib/popwatch.js (outside-click / Esc / key hand-over) internals, and the exact sizes of tokens (--lg-*) in ui/shared/glass-light.css: named by token only.
- `sys.stats` and `net.probe` implementations (main side) not inventoried.
- ui/desktop/mock-mantle.js skipped as instructed.
### Wallpapers window (renderer)
- Window size / min size / position / taskbar presence and where it opens from (launcher "Wallpapers", tray menu, `mantle-open wallpapers` arg): defined in lib/wallpaper.js#openLibrary (main lane).
- Exact listing schema fields (health, runtime, playback, rules.lines, lively.report) and the wording main produces for reasons: main lane.
- Detailed CSS metrics beyond those listed (paddings, radii, chip sizes); read only the theme/motion/layout parts of wallpapers.css.
- Whether the desktop page (ui/desktop or wall window) is what sends `props`/`sysinfo`/`cursor`/`pause` to the shim, and its cadence: main/desktop lane.
### Wallpaper engine (main process)
- "Restore Windows wallpaper / lock screen on exit": no such code found in lib/wallpaper*.js, lib/look-desktop.js, main.js (grep for restore near wallpaper/lock found nothing). Windows wallpaper is changed and left; the coordinator may want to confirm with the look-sync lane or treat it as not a feature.
- "Per-display" wallpaper: not present; everything is primary-display only (rows WALL-054, WALL-178).
- Lively hand-over/restore of Lively's own process: not present (row WALL-147).
- lib/look-desktop.js covered only for the Windows/lock-screen match and wait/adopt flow (WALL-191..200); its look.json publish record fields and top-band guard (lib/look-guard.js) are not inventoried here; belongs to a look-sync lane.
- ui/desktop wall player internals (element mounting, decoder-lost remount logic) inventoried only via wall-player.test.js titles; the desktop UI lane owns them.
- lib/features/_wall-window.js page half (ui/shared/wall-bdrop.js) not inventoried (other lane).
### Hot corners
- Settings > Hot corners select styling and layout (another lane owns the Settings controls); only behaviour is listed here.
- The tray menu enable/disable item itself and the Settings feature on/off toggle (features/tray lanes).
- Exact rendering of the welcome-page corners SVG/CSS (welcome lane).
- lib/bar-hover-main.test.js stubs `HotCorners.claims` to false; whether any test drives main.js hoverTick with a real claim was not confirmed.
- Toasts/screenshot/clips use of lib/corner.js beyond the registry API (their own lanes).
### Look (theme, accent, look.json, guard)
- ui/topbar/glass.js / topbar.js consumption of `topbar.guard` beyond the payload check (glassSettle, tint application): the topbar lane owns it.
- Settings > Personalization > Colors controls (Mantle appearance row, "Match Helm and Quartz" switch, glass sliders, accent picker, wording like "Following Windows: <x> right now"): Settings lane.
- lib/wall-still.js, lib/gif-frame.js, wallpaper.js internals and bar/launcher backdrop refresh logic: only their contracts with this subsystem are listed.
- lib/recover.e2e.test.js was matched by grep for the fallback toast but not opened; its exact coverage of LOOK-063/064 is unverified.
- No "auto/schedule" theme mode exists in lib/theme.js at this baseline (only system/light/dark); if a schedule lives elsewhere it was not found in scope.
